1111. ELIZA HEALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of June, 3 blankets, value 4s.; 1 pair of pillow-cases, value 6d.; 1 pair of sheets, value 3s.; 2 pillows, value 5s.; 1 bolster, value 6d.; 1 yard of green baize, value 1s.; 1 tea-tray, value 2s.; 1 pair of bellows, value 1s. 6d.; 1 hearth-rug, value 7s.; 1 fender, value 10s.; and 1 looking-glass, value 2s., the goods of Benjamin Astwick.
SARAH ASTWICK. I am the wife of Benjamin Astwick, and live in Popham-street, Islington. On the 12th of March I let the prisoner a first-floor front room - the property stated was in that room - she lived there with a man - they were to pay me 4s. 6d. a week - they paid me all but one week - on the 13th of June I went into their room, as they were fighting, and crying "Murder" - I then missed this property - the officer found the duplicates in the drawers in their room.
CHARLES HANES (police-constable N 75). I found the duplicates, which led to the pawnbrokers.
WILLIAM WEST. I am a pawnbroker - I have a fender, a hearth-rug, and six other articles, pawned by the prisoner.
GEORGE BARBER. I am a pawnbroker - I have two blankets, pawned on the 12th of March, by the prisoner.
JOSEPH WOLSTENHOLME. I am a pawnbroker - I have two pillows, pawned in the name of Mary Healey - I do not know by whom.
FREDERICK GILES. I am a pawnbroker - I have a blanket pawned at our shop - I cannot say by whom.
Prisoner's Defence. The prosecutor gave me leave to do any thing I liked in the room, so long as I kept my payments good.
SARAH ASTWICK. I never did - it is quite false.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18340703-219]
1112. WILLIAM KELLY and CHARLES KELLY were indicted for stealing, on the 21st of June, 1 handkerchief, value 2d., the goods of Samuel Shervill .
SAMUEL SHERVILL. I was in Shoreditch on Saturday, the 21st of June, and my pocket was picked - I do not know by whom; but I lost my handkerchief.
THOMAS TOOLE. I am a boot and shoe-maker - I was in Shoreditch on the 21st of June - I saw the prosecutor and the two prisoners - they tried his pockets once or twice - he then crossed over to Church-street, and went into a wine vaults - they waited till he came out, and he went down New Inn-yard - they followed him; and Charles Kelly took the handkerchief out of his pocket - I told him, and we followed, but could not find him - the next night, I saw Charles Kelly, and had him taken - they were both together when the handkerchief was stolen.
Cross-examined by Mr. CHURCHILL. Q. Is this your first case this session? A. Yes; I was here last session in a case of one of the swell mob, for taking a gentleman's watch - I was the only person who saw it done - I do not know how often I have been here as a witness against boys, for robbing persons unknown - I have never been turned out of Court - I never was refused my expenses - I may have gone away without them.
Q. Will you swear you have not been refused your expenses here? A. I never was but once - I will swear I have not twice - I have gone away once or twice without them - I do not recollect having a case of two boys before Mr. Common Sergeant Denman - I was not turned out ofCourt, unless the witnesses were ordered out - I was not turned out after the case was over.
Q. Do you remember last Session being reminded of a case in a Session before, and swearing to Mr. Phillips that he bullied you out of it? A. I used those words, or something to that effect - I cannot recollect whether Mr. Lee had been counsel in that case.
TIMOTHY RYAN (police-constable G 153). I took the prisoner from the information of the last witness.
Witness for the Defence.
- BRIGHTWELL. The witness Tool took my boy by mistake, and then let him go, and said the prisoner Charles was the boy - I was with my boy at the time - I had not been five minutes from my master's house - the policeman asked Toole if he was sure - he said he had no doubt of it - I said, "If you go, I will go with you," - and before we got to the London Apprentice, he took the prisoner Charles, and said, "Let the other go, he knows nothing of it."
THOMAS TOOLE re-examined. Q. How can you explain this? A. On the Sunday night, I saw Charles Kelly turn down a little court, and I went back for a policeman - I said to him, "There is a young fellow gone down here that robbed a person of his handkerchief last night" - I described him quite fully - we went down together, and saw this man's boy, and took him - he had a cap on his head, and I thought he was the boy - I brought him out, and a mob came round - the prisoner then came out and I said, "Take him, that is the boy."
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18340703-220]
1113. THOMAS HALLAM was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of July, 3 silver spoons, value 7s.; the goods of Thomas Ayres.
THOMAS AYRES. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in Bath-street, City-road. The prisoner was my errand boy for about a fortnight - we missed three spoons, one at a time - part of one was found at a refiner's, and one on the prisoner.
EDWARD ALLISON. I am apprentice to the prosecutor. He missed three spoons - I accused the prisoner of having one - he denied it; but he pulled this one from his breeches pocket.
JOHN JEFFRYS. I am a refiner. A boy (who I think was the prisoner) brought a broken spoon to our house, which was placed among other silver - I asked him his mother's name, and he told me a name which corresponded with the name on the spoon.(The prisoner received a good character.)
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Recommended to mercy by the Prosecutor and Jury. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18340703-221]
1114. MARY ASHTON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of July, 2 gowns, value 5s.: 2 pinafores, value 3s.; 1 pelerine, value 1s.; 6 shells, value 1s.; 1 cap, value 2s.; 1 half-sovereign; and 40 shillings; the goods and monies of Elizabeth Gull, her mistress.
JOSEPH WINNEY GULL. The prisoner was servant to my mother, Elizabeth Gull - she keeps the Duke of Marlborough public-house - she was with her three months, and my mother gave her warning to leave - on the day she was going, she brought her box (which was locked) down to the ground floor - my mother said it was her custom to search the boxes of her servants, and the prisoner gave her the key - she opened it, and the property was found - the prisoner said she was very sorry, and thought there was no harm in taking these things - the money was not in the box - I told the officer we had better search her person - she resisted very much; but we searched, and found on her 2l. 16s. - she said, "You cannot prove that is yours" - she was then taken to the station-house - she asked me if I would forgive her - I said it was not in my power.
ELIZA SARAH GULL. I am the prosecutrix's daughter. I was present when the prisoner's trunk was opened, and the property found - we had not missed it, nor the money - she said several times that she did not think there was any harm in taking the things; they are but of small value - my mother never gave them to her.
ELLEN EARLS. I lived at the prosecutrix's. I saw the prisoner at the closet where the cash box was kept, and heard the money rattle; but I cannot say that she took any - when she went to bed, she told me there were six cakes in her mistress's closet.
( William Hitchin, a silk and trimming-manufacturer; Sarah Smith; and Mrs. Howard, gave the prisoner a good character.)
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Recommended to mercy by the Prosecutrix and Jury. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18340703-222]
1115. JOHN SIMMS and THOMAS WOOLBARD WHITTON were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of July, 1 bracelet, value 2s., the goods of Robert Parker, from the person of Frederick Parker .
MARY BILLINGSLEY. I am nursemaid in the family of Mr. Robert Parker. On the 1st of July, I had the care of his child, Frederick Parker - I was in Kingsland-road, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon - he had a bracelet on - it was snatched off suddenly - I turned and saw the two prisoners - I called, "Stop thief;" and they ran - I pursued, and the policeman took them just as I came up to them.
GEORGE BONNEY. I was there, and saw Billingsley with the child - I saw the two prisoners - one of them made a snatch at the bracelet - they ran away, and I pursued them - I did not see the bracelet found.
WILLIAM NORTON. I was going along the road, at a quarter before five o'clock, and picked up this bracelet in the road the prisoners ran.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN GREEN (police-constable N 214). I heard the alarm, saw the prisoners, and took them.
SIMMS - GUILTY. Aged 14.
WHITTON - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18340703-223]
1116. JOHN WRIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of June, 1 bird-cage, value 1s., the goods of Frederick Smith Butler.
JAMES ALLEN. I know the prosecutor's shop - he is a baker - a bird and cage hung in front of his house on the 2nd of June - I saw the prisoner go and take it as I wasstanding at my father's door - I had not known him before - there was another person with him - the prisoner tied two sticks together and took the cage - he lowered it down, and the other took it - they watched the policeman's back being turned - it was just after four o'clock in the morning.
Prisoner. Q. When I came to you, the night I was taken, what did you say? A. I said I did not know you, but I would see in the morning; and when I saw you in the morning, I was sure it was you.
TIMOTHY TOOMEY (police-constable H 32). I received information, but could not find the prisoner till the 19th of June, when I met him with these two sticks. I took him to Allen, who said, "I do not know you now," but in the morning he knew him - the bird and cage have not been found.
FREDERICK WILLIAM BUTLER. I had a linnet in a cage, which I have lost.
Prisoner. Q. How high was your bird? A. Over the window, about ten feet from the ground.
Prisoner. Was not that enough to make any body a thief? I was going down the Hackney-road, when I was taken - the Lord above is my protector.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
